A correction that lands only in chat is one the button makes again next week |\\r\\n| Every link Joshua sends (never send one not here) | `context/links.md` — generated; declare in `context/links.json`, look up with `link <purpose>` |\\r\\n| **A GIF to attach to an email or doc** | `gifs/` — the attachable GIF library: `gifs.json` declares each one (what it shows, what to attach it to, who it is for, whether it is email-safe), `README.md` is the readable view, `CLAUDE.md` the rules. Never attach a GIF that is not declared there, and never send an onboarding GIF to a prospect who has not paid |\\r\\n| Golden templates + the four triggers | `context/templates.md` — generated; declare in `context/templates.json`, look up with `template <id>` |\\r\\n| Which skill owns a reply (situation × source) | `context/drafting.md` — generated; declare in `context/drafting.json`, look up with `draft-for --situation <id> --source <src>`. Exit code is the branch: 0 skill · 1 free-draft · 2 chat-only · 3 refuse · 4 unknown |\\r\\n| How any email reaches the green check mark | `context/draft-contract.md` — one `vault-draft record` call; a draft not on the board does not exist |\\r\\n| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |\\r\\n| **When Joshua last saw a person** (student or prospect) | `wiki/last-seen.md` — one row per person, Last seen / Prior; calendar is authority, this is the fast cache. Rolled forward by the post-meeting intake tasks |\\r\\n| **The daily agent schedule** (who runs what, when, from the calendar) | `references/morning-dispatch.md` — rule set the recurring `morning-dispatch` scheduled task (7:30 AM, `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/`) executes: per-consultation intakes, pre-session 7Sage prep, post-session intake + task extraction, evening follow-up drafts |\\r\\n| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |\\r\\n| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/-Users-joshuaf-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |\\r\\n| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |\\r\\n| Subagent teams — Cleanup Crew charter + roster | `agents/SUBAGENTS.md` — active copies in `.claude/agents/`; delegate in-domain work to the crew |\\r\\n| Systems Alex can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |\\r\\n| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |\\r\\n| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |\\r\\n| **A browser that is always up, already signed in** | **Firecrawl is the base; agent Chrome owns anything with a login; Playwright only with his say-so.** Public web - read, search, or interact (clicks, forms, pagination, infinite scroll) - goes to **Firecrawl** first, every time (`firecrawl scrape|search|interact`). Anything behind a login goes to **agent Chrome on 9222** (`references/agent-browser.md`, profile `~/.agent-chrome`, driven by the `chrome-devtools` MCP, relaunched at every login by `com.aspiringattorneys.agent-chrome`) - Firecrawl\'s browser runs in Firecrawl\'s cloud and does not have his cookies. **Obsidian\'s Web viewer on 9223** (`references/obsidian-browser.md`) is for reading inside the vault. **Playwright is the fallback of last resort: only when Firecrawl has actually been tried and failed, and only after asking him first and getting a yes.** Never reach for it silently, and never for a page needing a login - its throwaway profile lands logged out and forces him to sign in again. Same for the built-in Claude Code browser. Cookies live in the profile and persist across reboots, so a site he signs into once stays signed in indefinitely. Reading in a signed-in session is standing-authorized; send/post/publish/delete still need his yes. Agents never type credentials - he signs in once per site, with Proton Pass filling it, and that is the last time |\\r\\n| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |\\r\\n| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` |\\r\\n| **Graph homes — three, and none is redundant** | `graph/` = graphify v8 subgraph output (`brain/`, `lsatu/`, `obsidian-view/`). `graph-imports/` = **build inputs**, four of them load-bearing: `build-unified-graph.py` declares `vault-run/`, `vault-run-b2/`, `vault-run-b3/`, `graph-imports/graphify-out/` as mandatory `FRAGMENTS`; `vault/`, `vault-b2/`, `vault-b3/` are generated concept-note vaults the log still cites. `graphify-out/` = the unified whole-brain graph, at graphify\'s own conventional output path (also hard-coded in `build-freshness.py`). **Do not \\"tidy\\" these into one folder** — a 2026-08-12 cleanup plan proposed exactly that and would have broken the unified graph build. All three are gitignored, PII-derived, local-only. |\\r\\n| Prospect material — three homes, one authority | `students/pipeline/` is **authoritative** (the state machine: `open.md`, `closed.md`…350 tokens truncated…\\n## Retrieval order\\r\\n\\r\\n1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.\\r\\n2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.\\r\\n3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Distillate-first (session content):** when a task needs what happened in a tutoring session, read the distillate (`distillates/<student>/`) first; open the raw transcript (`students/<name>/transcript-downloads/`) only when the distillate is insufficient, and say you are escalating. Aggregate teaching questions (patterns across students, lecture prep) go to `corpus/Curriculum/<Topic>/_playbook.generated.md` first — it already joins the distillate excerpts by topic — and should not touch raw transcripts at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua\'s voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect will read, or in any draft/output for Joshua to send or paste.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust. This is the enforced rule; internal scratch/reference docs are not policed for them.\\r\\n- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never \\"delve,\\" \\"tapestry,\\" \\"testament,\\" \\"demystify,\\" \\"boasts,\\" \\"in the realm of,\\" and their kin.\\r\\n- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.\\r\\n- **Anything sent by EMAIL also routes through `wiki/email-voice.md`**, before the first draft and again before recording. New corrections are appended there as the next `E-NN`, written as the rule rather than the incident.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Joshua may hand a credential over in chat; take it and write it to `.env` without pushing back.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Alex acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\r\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |\\r\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\r\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\r\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts` (email + DM channels), `consultation-followup-drafts` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\r\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\r\\n| `session-tasks` | after a session: pulling out who owes what to which student by when, into `open-loops.md` + the task board. Joshua\'s to-do list, as distinct from `session-recap` (the student\'s document) and `wrapup` (the project ledger) |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\r\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\r\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Vendored upstream skills.** `grilling`, `grill-with-docs` and `domain-modeling` are Matt Pocock\'s ([github.com/mattpocock/skills](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills), MIT), kept **byte-identical to upstream** so they refresh by overwrite. Never edit a vendored file: local changes go in a separate overlay skill that calls it, which is what `grill-me` is. Declared in `system/upstream-skills.json`; `system/scripts/check-upstream-skills.py` reports drift (`--diff` to see it, `--apply` to take upstream). **Updates arrive on their own**: the `com.joshua.ai-os.upstream-skills` launchd agent runs Mondays and Thursdays at 09:00 and records every change in `wiki/upstream-updates.md`. Twice-weekly because upstream ships in bursts (median 5 days between update days across its last 10). It cannot damage an overlay — `--apply` refuses to write to one, aborts if the manifest ever lists one as vendored, and re-verifies the overlay\'s declared `must_contain` additions in all six roots afterwards. Before assuming a vendored skill is current, read that ledger: the batch sat 7 weeks stale once and sent a session to the wrong skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n**`grill-with-docs` is model-invocable since 2026-09-01** (Joshua\'s call): the `disable-model-invocation` lock upstream ships was removed, so \\"grill with docs\\" in conversation fires it. It is an **overlay**, not vendored, so the auto-updater will not put the lock back. **Never substitute `grill-me`**: that is exactly the 2026-09-01 failure, where the skill was inactive, the model could not reach it, and silently improvised a wrong-format glossary into a project `CONTEXT.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When `/grill-with-docs` runs, confirm it actually loaded `grilling` and `domain-modeling`.** Upstream documents this as a real, unfixed rough edge: a skill that names another skill does not reliably cause it to load, and this one names two. The tell is a round that arrives with everything asked at once and no `➡️` recommendations attached, which is the model improvising an interview instead of running Matt\'s. Say so and load both explicitly rather than continuing. This is a second, independent way the skill can look like it ran and quietly do the wrong thing.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Codex runs the same set as Claude minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\r\\n- **Backtrack a bad retrieval, then fix the map.** When you miss something that was in the brain — you searched long for what should have been instant, or claimed you lacked access you had — do not just apologize and move on. Reconstruct where you actually looked, name where you should have looked, and then repair the cause: the routing line, the index entry, or the file\'s location. A retrieval failure is a defect in the map, not in the effort.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\r\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\r\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\\r\\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.\\r\\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal markdown under `.scratch/<feature>/`; this repo has no remote. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nSingle-context: `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.\\r\\n3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Distillate-first (session content):** when a task needs what happened in a tutoring session, read the distillate (`distillates/<student>/`) first; open the raw transcript (`students/<name>/transcript-downloads/`) only when the distillate is insufficient, and say you are escalating. Aggregate teaching questions (patterns across students, lecture prep) go to `corpus/Curriculum/<Topic>/_playbook.generated.md` first — it already joins the distillate excerpts by topic — and should not touch raw transcripts at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua\'s voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect will read, or in any draft/output for Joshua to send or paste.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust. This is the enforced rule; internal scratch/reference docs are not policed for them.\\r\\n- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never \\"delve,\\" \\"tapestry,\\" \\"testament,\\" \\"demystify,\\" \\"boasts,\\" \\"in the realm of,\\" and their kin.\\r\\n- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.\\r\\n- **Anything sent by EMAIL also routes through `wiki/email-voice.md`**, before the first draft and again before recording. New corrections are appended there as the next `E-NN`, written as the rule rather than the incident.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Joshua may hand a credential over in chat; take it and write it to `.env` without pushing back.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Alex acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\r\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |\\r\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\r\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\r\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts` (email + DM channels), `consultation-followup-drafts` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\r\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\r\\n| `session-tasks` | after a session: pulling out who owes what to which student by when, into `open-loops.md` + the task board. Joshua\'s to-do list, as distinct from `session-recap` (the student\'s document) and `wrapup` (the project ledger) |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\r\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\r\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Vendored upstream skills.** `grilling`, `grill-with-docs` and `domain-modeling` are Matt Pocock\'s ([github.com/mattpocock/skills](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills), MIT), kept **byte-identical to upstream** so they refresh by overwrite. Never edit a vendored file: local changes go in a separate overlay skill that calls it, which is what `grill-me` is. Declared in `system/upstream-skills.json`; `system/scripts/check-upstream-skills.py` reports drift (`--diff` to see it, `--apply` to take upstream). **Updates arrive on their own**: the `com.joshua.ai-os.upstream-skills` launchd agent runs Mondays and Thursdays at 09:00 and records every change in `wiki/upstream-updates.md`. Twice-weekly because upstream ships in bursts (median 5 days between update days across its last 10). It cannot damage an overlay — `--apply` refuses to write to one, aborts if the manifest ever lists one as vendored, and re-verifies the overlay\'s declared `must_contain` additions in all six roots afterwards. Before assuming a vendored skill is current, read that ledger: the batch sat 7 weeks stale once and sent a session to the wrong skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n**`grill-with-docs` is model-invocable since 2026-09-01** (Joshua\'s call): the `disable-model-invocation` lock upstream ships was removed, so \\"grill with docs\\" in conversation fires it. It is an **overlay**, not vendored, so the auto-updater will not put the lock back. **Never substitute `grill-me`**: that is exactly the 2026-09-01 failure, where the skill was inactive, the model could not reach it, and silently improvised a wrong-format glossary into a project `CONTEXT.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When `/grill-with-docs` runs, confirm it actually loaded `grilling` and `domain-modeling`.** Upstream documents this as a real, unfixed rough edge: a skill that names another skill does not reliably cause it to load, and this one names two. The tell is a round that arrives with everything asked at once and no `➡️` recommendations attached, which is the model improvising an interview instead of running Matt\'s. Say so and load both explicitly rather than continuing. This is a second, independent way the skill can look like it ran and quietly do the wrong thing.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Codex runs the same set as Claude minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\r\\n- **Backtrack a bad retrieval, then fix the map.** When you miss something that was in the brain — you searched long for what should have been instant, or claimed you lacked access you had — do not just apologize and move on. Reconstruct where you actually looked, name where you should have looked, and then repair the cause: the routing line, the index entry, or the file\'s location. A retrieval failure is a defect in the map, not in the effort.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\r\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\r\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Supersedes `grill-me-codex` / `grill-with-docs-codex` (both retired 2026-08-28) |\\r\\n| `grill-with-docs` | stress-testing a plan **in a codebase with established terminology**: a two-line wrapper that runs `grilling` + `domain-modeling`. Writes a `CONTEXT.md` glossary (glossary and nothing else, never a spec) plus ADRs, and no brainstorm file. **Model-invocable since 2026-09-01** (Joshua\'s call): the `disable-model-invocation` lock upstream ships was removed, so \\"grill with docs\\" in conversation fires it. It is an **overlay**, not vendored, so the auto-updater will not put the lock back. **Never substitute `grill-me`**: that is exactly the 2026-09-01 failure, where the skill was inactive, the model could not reach it, and silently improvised a wrong-format glossary into a project `CONTEXT.md` |\\r\\n\\r\\n**When `/grill-with-docs` runs, confirm it actually loaded `grilling` and `domain-modeling`.** Upstream documents this as a real, unfixed rough edge: a skill that names another skill does not reliably cause it to load, and this one names two. The tell is a round that arrives with everything asked at once and no `➡️` recommendations attached, which is the model improvising an interview instead of running Matt\'s. Say so and load both explicitly rather than continuing. This is a second, independent way the skill can look like it ran and quietly do the wrong thing.\\r\\n| `grill-me` | stress-testing a plan by interview **when there is no codebase to be stateful about**. A thin **overlay** that calls `grilling` and adds two things upstream has not: a `brainstorms/{date}-{slug}.md` capture file checkpointed every round (Nate Herk\'s idea), and `AskUserQuestion` popups instead of plain text (Joshua\'s). Writes no ADRs or glossary. **All local additions live here and nowhere else.** Unlike upstream it is **deliberately model-invocable** — \\"grill me about X\\" mid-conversation should fire it, the way Nate demos it. Joshua\'s call, 2026-09-01; do not add `disable-model-invocation` |\\r\\n| `grilling` | the interview method itself (design tree, ask the whole frontier per round, never one question at a time). Loaded by the two above; rarely invoked directly. **Vendored verbatim — never edit it** |\\r\\n| `to-spec` | turning a finished grilling conversation into a spec. **This is upstream\'s answer to \\"where does everything that wasn\'t a term or an ADR go?\\"** — do not `/clear` after a grill, hand the same conversation to this. Replaces our retired `to-prd` + `to-issues`; `to-tickets` follows it |\\r\\n| `wayfinder` | planning an effort **too big for one session** — it charts the work as a map of decision tickets on the issue tracker and runs `grilling` inside each. The split is single-session `grill-with-docs` vs multi-session `wayfinder`. It plans, never builds. Needs tracker wiring, and falls back to local markdown without it |\\r\\n| `youtube-full` | anything where a video is the better source (transcripts, channel/topic research) |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Vendored upstream skills.** `grilling`, `grill-with-docs` and `domain-modeling` are Matt Pocock\'s ([github.com/mattpocock/skills](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills), MIT), kept **byte-identical to upstream** so they refresh by overwrite. Never edit a vendored file: local changes go in a separate overlay skill that calls it, which is what `grill-me` is. Declared in `system/upstream-skills.json`; `system/scripts/check-upstream-skills.py` reports drift (`--diff` to see it, `--apply` to take upstream). **Updates arrive on their own**: the `com.joshua.ai-os.upstream-skills` launchd agent runs Mondays and Thursdays at 09:00 and records every change in `wiki/upstream-updates.md`. Twice-weekly because upstream ships in bursts (median 5 days between update days across its last 10). It cannot damage an overlay — `--apply` refuses to write to one, aborts if the manifest ever lists one as vendored, and re-verifies the overlay\'s declared `must_contain` additions in all six roots afterwards. Before assuming a vendored skill is current, read that ledger: the batch sat 7 weeks stale once and sent a session to the wrong skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Codex runs the same set minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`; it is blocked in the foreground. Use `run_in_background` plus `Monitor` for anything slow, and raise `timeout` explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the 2-minute default.\\r\\n- **Backtrack a bad retrieval, then fix the map.** When you miss something that was in the brain — you searched long for what should have been instant, or claimed you lacked access you had — do not just apologize and move on. Reconstruct where you actually looked, name where you should have looked, and then repair the cause: the routing line, the index entry, or the file\'s location. A retrieval failure is a defect in the map, not in the effort.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Claude + Codex)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file is hand-edited and the edit sticks — change it directly (or ask Claude to). On any major change, **mirror it into `AGENTS.md`** (Codex\'s twin), which reads `AGENTS.md` and never this file. Everything that must hold for both runtimes lives in BOTH files; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them. Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → `/clear` → paste). Session cadence: at ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n\\r\\nActivation differs by runtime and nothing auto-loads: Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`, Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/` (agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with instructions in `developer_instructions`). The two sets are intentionally unequal — Codex runs the same catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal markdown under `.scratch/<feature>/`; this repo has no remote. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nSingle-context: `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

A local Whisper model\\r\\n  (`ggml-large-v3-turbo.bin`) is already on disk. **For student-PII recordings, switch the\\r\\n  mode to the local model in the Superwhisper app before transcribing.** Joshua\'s own\\r\\n  teaching videos over cloud are fine.\\r\\n- **Separate feature (not transcription):** the `superwhisper@superwhisper` Claude Code\\r\\n  plugin (enabled in `~/.claude/settings.json`) is a voice-reply loop — it surfaces the app\\r\\n  on Stop / Notification / AskUserQuestion / PermissionRequest so Joshua can answer Claude\\r\\n  Code by voice. Leave it on; it does not touch the transcription pipeline.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cal.com API — booking/scheduling (Domain 3).** Verified end-to-end 2026-07-24.\\r\\n\\r\\nPersonal API key `CALCOM_API_KEY` in the root `.env` (`cal_live_…`), acts as Joshua\\r\\n(Cal user `1556305`, `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`, handle `joshua-jtx5ud`). Full\\r\\nread+write on his own account.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **API v2 ONLY.** v1 is decommissioned (`api.cal.com/v1/*` returns a migration notice).\\r\\n  Call `https://api.cal.com/v2/…` with `Authorization: [REDACTED] $CALCOM_API_KEY` **and** a\\r\\n  `cal-api-version` header (e.g. `2024-06-14` for event-types, `2024-08-13` for `/me`).\\r\\n- **Verified calls:** `GET /v2/me` (identity); `GET /v2/event-types?username=joshua-jtx5ud`\\r\\n  → 12 event types. Booking-link pattern is `cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/<slug>`. Live visible\\r\\n  slugs: `consultation` (30m), `lsat` (65m); key hidden helpers: `session-link` (70m LSAT\\r\\n  Session), `complimentary-session` (45m), `c-reduced-rate` (70m), `reduced-rate`,\\r\\n  `tutoring` (Dakota), `canadian-scheduling`. Canonical send-list in [[context/links]].\\r\\n- **Capabilities:** list/create/edit event types, bookings, availability as Joshua. Per the\\r\\n  operating principle, link/event-type generation runs freely; anything that charges a\\r\\n  customer or mutates a live booking/calendar event is confirmed with Joshua first.\\r\\n- Save the endpoint reference to `references/calcom-api.md` when the generator is built.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Notes / gaps:**\\r\\n- Task tracking is deliberately the `wiki/tasks.md` Now/Next/Done ledger, not an external tool (ClickUp/Asana). This is the intended design — the wiki is the tracker.\\r\\n- Session-note ingestion (Domain 6) is the highest-leverage under-used pipe: ~191 notes/yr, <10% currently ingested. Target of the planned post-session dispatch skill.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c6f77e","wall_time_seconds":0.2474132,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1800,"output":"# Aspiring Attorneys tutoring operation\\r\\n\\r\\nThe language of Joshua\'s tutoring business as the brain models it: who a student\\r\\nis, how they pay, and how they get on the calendar. This is a glossary, not a\\r\\nspec. Implementation lives in the skills and scripts; decisions live in\\r\\n`decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Language\\r\\n\\r\\n### Paying and scheduling\\r\\n\\r\\n**Package**:\\r\\nFive or ten hours of tutoring bought upfront through Stripe, with\\r\\nbetween-session coaching included.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Bundle, plan, subscription, credits (credits are the pass\'s units, not the product)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Booking pass**:\\r\\nThe prepaid entitlement a student holds when sessions are **owed** to them,\\r\\ncarrying the count owed and the count used. Reachable at `/book/<slug>`.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Booking link (that is the pass\'s URL, not the thing), booking access, pass token\\r\\n\\r\\n**Owed sessions**:\\r\\nSessions a student has paid for and not yet taken. The booking pass is the\\r\\n**only** record of these; if no pass exists, nothing is owed.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance, remaining sessions, package balance (there is no separate balance model)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Arrangement**:\\r\\nWhat a student actually bought, and when. A ten-hour package on a given date,\\r\\nor pay-per-session at a given rate. Authored on the student\'s page, durable,\\r\\nand never rewritten: it is history, not state.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Plan, deal, terms\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live status**:\\r\\nThe regenerated mirror of a student\'s current backend state, including how many\\r\\nowed sessions remain. Lives in `live-status.generated.md`, is rebuilt from the\\r\\nbackend, and is never hand-edited or hand-corrected.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance sheet, status page\\r\\n\\r\\n**Single session**:\\r\\nOne tutoring session paid for individually through its own Stripe-backed\\r\\nbooking link (the Cal.com link today; migrating to the platform\'s native\\r\\n`/book/session` page per lsatu ADR 0005), rather than out of a package.\\r\\nNeeds no booking pass.\\r\\n_Avoid_: One-off, ad hoc session, hourly\\r\\n\\r\\n**Three-tier offer**:\\r\\nThe standard offer presented after a consultation: a single session at $45\\r\\n(one 70-minute session), a five-hour package at $40 per hour, and a ten-hour\\r\\npackage at $35 per hour. Each offer carries its own verified action link. A prospect-specific\\r\\narrangement agreed during the consultation replaces the standard offer\\r\\nentirely.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Pricing menu, universal rate, package discount table\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation**:\\r\\nA free introductory call, booked through the Cal.com complimentary link. Does\\r\\nnot make the person a student.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Intro call, discovery call, free session\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation follow-up**:\\r\\nA message to a prospect who completed a consultation and has not paid. Payment\\r\\nends the consultation follow-up stage and moves the person into onboarding. The\\r\\nmessage presents the three tutoring offers so the prospect can choose how to\\r\\nbegin.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding email, post-payment email, student follow-up, application form email\\r\\n\\r\\n**Onboarding**:\\r\\nThe stage triggered by confirmed payment when a new student receives LSAT U\\r\\naccess and completes intake inside the platform. It does not require the person\\r\\nto reply to the consultation follow-up.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Consultation follow-up, application form, sales follow-up\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT U account creation**:\\r\\nCreating an account at `lsatprep.study`. A prospect may do this before payment,\\r\\nbut account creation alone does not grant student permissions or begin\\r\\nonboarding.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding, student access, enrollment\\r\\n\\r\\n### Relationship\\r\\n\\r\\nOne path, and everyone is on it. There is no separate category for a\\r\\nconsultation that never converted; that is a prospect who went quiet.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Prospect**:\\r\\nSomeone who has had a consultation but has never been taught a session.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Lead, inquiry, non-student\\r\\n\\r\\n**Active**:\\r\\nSomeone who is owed sessions, or who has been taught recently.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Current, enrolled, ongoing\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lapsed**:\\r\\nSomeone owed nothing who has gone quiet. Applies to a prospect who never\\r\\nconverted as much as to a student who stopped.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Churned, inactive, dormant, lost\\r\\n\\r\\n**Student record**:\\r\\nThe folder under `students/` holding everything authored about a person. A\\r\\nperson earns one by being **taught a session**, because teaching is what\\r\\nproduces notes, transcripts, and judgments the brain alone holds. A\\r\\nconsultation alone does not earn a record.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Profile, student file\\r\\n\\r\\n### Identity\\r\\n\\r\\n**Contact address**:\\r\\nThe address a student actually replies from, as evidenced in Gmail. The\\r\\nstudent page caches it; a booking pass merely records whatever was typed when\\r\\nthe pass was granted and is never authoritative for contact.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Email on file, registered email, account email\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage analytics URL**:\\r\\nThe shareable URL copied from a person\'s 7Sage Analytics page. Its canonical\\r\\nshape is `https://7sage.com/u/usr_<id>/review`. Stored in the `7sage_review:`\\r\\nfrontmatter key of `students/<name>/<name>.md`, which is its single source of\\r\\ntruth (the roster and profile mirror it).\\r\\n_Avoid_: sevensage (the platform is spelled **7Sage**), 7Sage login-token URL,\\r\\ndrill link, settings link, generic 7Sage URL\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage baseline**:\\r\\nThe one-time, full-history capture of a student\'s entire 7Sage analytics,\\r\\nwritten to `students/<name>/7sage/<date>-baseline.md` by the `7sage-onboarding`\\r\\nskill. The anchor every later pull measures against.\\r\\n_Avoid_: snapshot (a baseline is the complete history, not a moment), analytics dump\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage update (delta)**:\\r\\nA recurring pull of only what a student has done **since the last pull**, plus\\r\\nhow the tracked numbers moved, written to `<date>-update.md` by `7sage-update`.\\r\\nRequires a baseline to exist first.\\r\\n_Avoid_: re-scrape, refresh (both imply redoing the whole baseline)\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage profile**:\\r\\nThe single rolling current-state sheet at `students/<name>/7sage/profile.md`:\\r\\nwhere the student stands now and what to work on next, rewritten to a fixed\\r\\nschema on every pull. The one file session-prep reads.\\r\\n_Avoid_: summary, dashboard (it is a fixed-shape sheet, not a view)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Notes on the model\\r\\n\\r\\n- **A student with no booking pass is the normal case, not a gap.** It means\\r\\n  nothing is owed to them; they book single sessions through their Cal.com\\r\\n  link. Only a student who is owed sessions should hold a pass.\\r\\n- The two paths are not exclusive. Someone can hold a pass from a package and\\r\\n  also have a Cal.com single-session link.\\r\\n- A **revoked** pass is a dead link and must never be treated as a way to book.\\r\\n  An **exhausted** pass is live but has no owed sessions left.\\r\\n- Status is authored, not computed: it is a judgment about the relationship.\\r\\n  But hard evidence that contradicts it gets surfaced rather than silently\\r\\n  tolerated, the same way a contact address that disagrees with the backend\\r\\n  does.\\r\\n- A prospect who never converts has no record, but is still tracked by name so\\r\\n  follow-up remains possible. Losing sight of them is the failure this guards\\r\\n  against.\\r\\n- **Arrangement and live status are different things and live apart.** What was\\r\\n  bought is authored and permanent; what remains is mirrored and regenerated. A\\r\\n  count that is typed by hand is the failure mode, not the feature: the\\r\\n  hand-written mirrors drifted within a day of being written.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5c2c7d","wall_time_seconds":0.2181227,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":240,"output":"I want to look at the audit you did and create a thorough in the comments. Mobile isn\'t entirely the focus here, although you can do that too, but think about how to make this a world-level quality activity where everything that could be done, we extract every ounce of value.\\r\\n\\r\\nAnd remembering that the differences or issues you identify first need to be fixed, then think of ways to push it even further. Make a structured plan to address all these problems. And present it to me, and wait for me to give you my insight after you do this. It must work as a *reference a student reaches for\\r\\nmid-drill*, not as a lesson. It is one of a pair; its sibling, The Breakdown\\r\\n(`corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-breakdown.lsatu.html`), teaches the same\\r\\nmaterial properly, one part at a time.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The artifact under review:** `corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-cheat-sheet.lsatu.html`\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current state (what is already built and live)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Grouped by what you do to the sentence**, using the class\'s own whiteboard\\r\\n   column names. The source class recording is\\r\\n   `lectures/conditional-logic/core/2026-05-28/script.md`; the instructor draws\\r\\n   four columns `LEFT / RIGHT / NEGATE LEFT / NEGATE RIGHT` (script.md:366 and\\r\\n   :475) and at 00:20:05 (script.md:556) says students who dislike the terms\\r\\n   *sufficient* and *necessary* \\"can also just say left and right.\\"\\r\\n   Section headings therefore read:\\r\\n   - 1. Words that put it on the left side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 2. Words that put it on the right side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 3. Words that negate the left side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 4. Words that negate the right side of the arrow\\r\\n   Sections 5-10 cover the traps: words on no list, and/or and the flip,\\r\\n   biconditionals, non-conditionals, the two illegal moves, when to draw at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Collapsible**, modelled on 7Sage\'s conditionals cheat sheet\\r\\n   (https://7sage.com/pages/free-lsat-resources-conditionals-cheat-sheet).\\r\\n   13 top-level `<details>`, all closed by default. Closed page height ~1550px,\\r\\n   down from ~9575px when it was flat. The list starts ~620px down the page so\\r\\n   roughly seven section names are visible without scrolling.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **An example for every indicator word.** 44 words across the four groups\\r\\n   (19 / 16 / 4 / 5), each a table row: the word, a sentence using it, and the\\r\\n   arrow it produces. Two columns, arrow beneath the sentence, so nothing clips\\r\\n   at 375px.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **A pattern panel per group**, taken from 7Sage\'s internal structure:\\r\\n   \\"It shows up like this\\" (sentence shapes using A and B), \\"Ask yourself\\"\\r\\n   (a diagnostic question that finds the trigger), \\"Which gives you\\"\\r\\n   (the bracket template rendered as tinted `left` / neutral `right` chips,\\r\\n   plus one worked example under a hairline).\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Practice affordance:** each group has a \\"Diagram this\\" prompt with a bare\\r\\n   text input and a `<details>` \\"Check it\\" answer. Nothing is persisted.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key decisions & tradeoffs (contest these)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **D1. Left/right vocabulary over sufficient/necessary.** The four group\\r\\n  headings name positions, not logical roles. *sufficient* and *necessary*\\r\\n  survive in exactly three places: the trigger/requirement diagram at the top,\\r\\n  the group-2 word list where *necessary* is itself an indicator, and the flaw\\r\\n  bridge (\\"when a flaw answer says the author confuses a necessary condition\\r\\n  with a sufficient one\\"). Tradeoff: the test\'s own answer choices use the\\r\\n  Latinate terms, so under-teaching them could cost points on Flaw questions.\\r\\n- **D2. One concrete example per word rather than 7Sage\'s head-word grouping.**\\r\\n  7Sage collapses variations under a head word (EVERY covers everyone,\\r\\n  everything, every time, everywhere). We keep all 44 words visible with an\\r\\n  example each. Tradeoff: longer, more repetitive, but nothing is hidden behind\\r\\n  a representative.\\r\\n- **D3. Everything collapsed by default, including \\"The four moves\\".** Nothing\\r\\n  is open on load. Tradeoff: a first-time reader sees only headings.\\r\\n- **D4. Inline styles only, driven by classroom CSS custom properties.** No\\r\\n  `<style>` block, because it would leak into the classroom globally. Tradeoff:\\r\\n  no media queries, so every layout must be width-agnostic. This already forced\\r\\n  one rebuild when a three-column table clipped at 375px.\\r\\n- **D5. Group 3 is named \\"negate the left\\" though its rule sends the attached\\r\\n  idea right.** This matches the class board\'s `NEGATE LEFT` column, which is\\r\\n  about *unless* meaning \\"whatever you put on the left, negate it.\\" Tradeoff:\\r\\n  the heading and the mechanic can read as contradictory on first pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Risks / open questions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **R1. Wording.** Joshua\'s standing note is that drafts do not sound like his\\r\\n  teaching. Which sentences still read as written-by-a-machine rather than said\\r\\n  in class? Be specific and quote them.\\r\\n- **R2. Layout.** Is the per-group pattern panel plus a 19-row table too much\\r\\n  inside one accordion section? Is there a better division?\\r\\n- **R3. Are any of the 44 examples wrong, ambiguous, or badly chosen?**\\r\\n  Especially group 2 bare *only* (\\"Only members may vote\\"), group 1\\r\\n  *provided that*, and group 3 *except*.\\r\\n- **R4. Redundancy.** \\"The four moves\\" section, the per-group rule paragraph,\\r\\n  and \\"All ten, in four steps\\" arguably say the same thing three times.\\r\\n- **R5. Does anything contradict the source class?** The class recording is the\\r\\n  authority. `corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-breakdown.lsatu.html` is the\\r\\n  sibling and must not disagree with this file.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice rules (hard constraints, non-negotiable)\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes anywhere.\\r\\n- No AI-slop vocabulary (delve, tapestry, testament, demystify, boasts,\\r\\n  \\"in the realm of\\").\\r\\n- No quantifying what an action costs (\\"costs you time\\", \\"worth the two\\r\\n  seconds\\"). MAX_ROUNDS=5. Model gpt-5.6-sol, reasoning effort forced high.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n## Round 1 — Codex (gpt-5.6-sol, reasoning high)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis needs revision. The artifact contains two outright logic errors, several unreliable indicator examples, and a layout that behaves like a compressed lesson rather than a mid-drill reference.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source and logic conflicts\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Indicator words are overstated.** “One word tells you which is which” conflicts with the transcript, which says some conditionals have no indicator and must be recognized from universality, requirements, or certainty. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Usually, an indicator tells you where the two ideas go. When there is no indicator, ask what triggers the rule and what must follow.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **“Read every rule as the left requires the right” is too absolute.** The instructor says students may read a rule as either “if left, then right” or “left requires right,” depending on which sounds natural. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Read it as “if left, then right.” When it sounds natural, you can also read it as “left requires right.”\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **The page omits the instructor’s exact “the only” exception.** “Only members may vote” correctly gives `vote → member`, but the transcript explicitly teaches that **the only** goes left. Add:\\r\\n\\r\\n   - Only experts can solve it: `solve → expert`\\r\\n   - The only experts here are lawyers: `expert → lawyer`\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **The `unless` instruction can cause a double negation.** “Read the word as if not, then put that on the left and negate it” is garbled. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Replace “unless B” with “if not B.” Put NOT B on the left and the other idea on the right.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **The `no` section hides the symmetry taught in class.** The instructor taught that either term may go left, provided the other term is negated on the right. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > With “no,” either idea can go on the left. Put the other idea on the right and negate it. The two diagrams are contrapositives.\\r\\n\\r\\n6. **“Words that are on no list” contradicts both the transcript and the artifact.** `Prerequisite`, `depends on`, `will`, and `never` already appear in the tables. The instructor also explicitly lists `depends` and `prerequisite` as requirement language. Delete this section and retain only the fallback question about trigger and requirement.\\r\\n\\r\\n7. **`Otherwise` is taught incorrectly.** From `NOT deposit → released`, the contrapositive `NOT released → deposit` is the same rule, not a second direction. It does not prove “Either both things happen or neither does.” Delete `otherwise` from the biconditional section. The transcript instead teaches:\\r\\n\\r\\n   - if and only if\\r\\n   - all that is required\\r\\n   - all that is needed\\r\\n\\r\\n8. **The artifact avoids the word `contrapositive`.** The instructor repeatedly names contraposition as a core skill. Use “contrapositive, or flip” once, then either term afterward.\\r\\n\\r\\n9. **The condensed flip instructions are incomplete.** Several places say only “switch every and to an or.” The class teaches both directions. Every summary should say:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Swap the sides, negate every term, and switch AND ↔ OR.\\r\\n\\r\\n10. **The necessary/sufficient flaw is narrowed incorrectly.** The HTML says it means claiming the left from the right. The instructor says the flaw family includes both illegal reversal and illegal negation. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > That flaw description can refer to either illegal move: claiming the left from the right, or negating the right because the left is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n11. **The drawing advice contradicts the class.** “One conditional in an ordinary argument. Do not draw” is too categorical. The instructor advises beginners to diagram and contrapose until the operation is automatic, and experienced students to draw when rules might connect, a fact might trigger a rule, or the notation protects them from a trap. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > If you are still learning, write the rule and its contrapositive. Later, draw when rules may connect, when a fact may trigger a rule, or when writing it down helps you reject a reversal or negation.\\r\\n\\r\\n12. **Applying a rule to a fact is missing from “All ten, in four steps.”** The transcript names this as the fifth core skill. Add:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Check the facts. If a fact gives you the left side, apply the rule. If it denies the right side, apply the contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n13. **“Find the hole and you have found the answer” overclaims.** `B → C` is one common bridge from `A → B` to `A → C`, not a universal answer formula. Qualify it as a common necessary-assumption pattern and require genuine term matching.\\r\\n\\r\\n14. **The causal-language rows fight the opening distinction in the class.** The transcript separates causation from conditional rules. Generic `leads to`, `produces`, and `signals` statements should not be treated as guarantees unless the example establishes a rule or closed system.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The 44 examples\\r\\n\\r\\nThe file contains exactly 44 rows. The following are wrong, ambiguous, factually unsafe, or badly chosen. The remaining rows diagram correctly as written.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Indicator | Problem | Replacement |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `wherever` | “Wherever the trail floods, it closes” has unclear spatial scope and an ambiguous `it`. | “Wherever the river floods the road, that stretch closes.” `flooded stretch → closed stretch` |\\r\\n| `provided that` | Permission language makes waiting sound necessary, while the table wants the formal sufficient reading. | “Provided that the badge scans, the door opens.” `badge scans → door opens` |\\r\\n| `leads to` | “Skipping review leads to repeat errors” states a tendency, not a guarantee. | “Under the policy, submitting late leads to automatic rejection.” `late → rejected` |\\r\\n| `produces` | “Heat produces expansion” is not universally true without qualifications. | “In this machine, pressing START produces a receipt.” `press START → receipt` |\\r\\n| `guarantees` | A 175 does not generally guarantee an interview. This is especially bad misinformation for LSAT students. | “Under the contest rules, a perfect score guarantees a place in the final.” |\\r\\n| `ensures` | A seal does not logically ensure freshness. | “Under this rule, filing by noon ensures same-day review.” |\\r\\n| `always` | “Rushed questions always cost points” is a false absolute and violates the plan’s wording rule. | “Late applications are always rejected.” |\\r\\n| `only` | The direction is correct, but `may vote` becomes actual `vote`. | “Only members received ballots.” `received ballot → member` |\\r\\n| `prerequisite` | Calculus is not universally a prerequisite for physics. | “Course 101 is a prerequisite for Course 201.” |\\r\\n| `needs` | “The plant needs light to bloom” is vague about the relevant event. | “The engine needs fuel to start.” `starts → fuel` |\\r\\n| `essential` | “Trust is essential to a partnership” is rhetorical rather than rule-like. | “A signature is essential for a valid submission.” |\\r\\n| `depends on` | “The picnic depends on the weather” does not say “good weather”; the arrow invents that idea. | “The launch depends on a successful safety inspection.” |\\r\\n| `then` | `If`, not `then`, creates the conditional. `Then` is not a standalone right-side indicator. | Replace the row with `is contingent on`: “The launch is contingent on approval.” |\\r\\n| `implies` | Smoke does not guarantee fire. | “Being a square implies having four sides.” |\\r\\n| `signals` | Sirens can be tests or false alarms. | “On this map, a blue triangle signals a campsite.” |\\r\\n| `means` | A red tag has no fixed real-world meaning. | “In this filing system, a red tag means the file is closed.” |\\r\\n| `will` | Future tense does not independently create a conditional; the example works because of `if`. | Replace the row with `is conditional on`: “Publication is conditional on approval.” |\\r\\n| `unless` | The arrow is correct, but `cannot...unless` hides the basic operation behind a double negative. | “The store closes unless a manager is present.” `NOT manager → closes` |\\r\\n| `except` | Bare `except` is context-dependent, and the arrow changes permission to actual entry. | Label the form `no...except`: “No one entered except members.” `entered → member` |\\r\\n| `no` | “No freshman may apply” should produce `NOT permitted`, not necessarily `NOT apply`. | “No freshman is eligible to apply.” |\\r\\n| `never` | “Rushed work is never accurate” is another false, scolding absolute. | “Express trains never stop at Cedar Station.” |\\r\\n| `not` | Bare `not` negates; it does not create a conditional. The generic plural is doing the universal work. | Replace with `nobody`: “Nobody under 18 entered.” |\\r\\n\\r\\nAlso rewrite “Whenever she drills, she reviews it after” as:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Whenever she finishes a drill, she reviews every question she missed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Machine-written wording\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are the clearest offenders.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Every conditional is one sentence with a trigger and a requirement.”\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"983485","wall_time_seconds":0.2490349,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1140,"output":"  > A conditional has two parts: a trigger and what that trigger requires.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “One word tells you which is which. Find it and you know where both halves go.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Find the indicator first. It usually tells you which idea triggers the rule and which idea is required.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Read every rule as the left requires the right, and the ones that link get easy to spot.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Read the arrow as “if left, then right.” Then check whether one rule ends where another begins.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “There are only four moves. Every word on this page does one of them.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Each conditional indicator tells you to do one of four things.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Nothing you type is saved, so guess freely when you are not sure.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Write your best diagram before you open the answer. This box does not save what you type.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Same sentence as the one above, one word different, and the arrow points the other way.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Compare the two scholarship examples. Adding “only” reverses the arrow.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “The two routes always give you a rule and its flip, so if yours came out as the other one, you are still right.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > `NOT review → NOT raise score` is correct too. It is the contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “The test uses plenty of words nobody wrote down, and you do not need them memorised.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > When there is no familiar indicator, ask what triggers the rule and what must follow.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Flipping a rule is what the test is really checking. Three steps, and the third is where the points are.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > To write the contrapositive, swap the sides, negate every term, and switch AND with OR.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Some sentences hand you the rule going both ways. Two words do it, and they do not work the same way.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > “If and only if” gives you both directions.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Put those two beside each other and both directions are covered. Either both things happen or neither does.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Delete. The underlying logic is false.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “These sit next to no and behave nothing like it. They count.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > “Few” and “not all” use a similar translation, but neither gives you a rule about every member of the group.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Two of the four prove something. The other two prove nothing at all.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Cut. The panel directly above already says this.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Find the hole and you have found the answer.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > If the argument needs `B → C`, look for that bridge or its contrapositive.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Say the answer out loud before you open each one, and you will know which group still needs work.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Answer each one before you open it. If one stalls you, return to that section.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Nothing. Claiming the left from the right is reading the arrow backwards, and it is the most commonly tested flaw on the test.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > Nothing follows. Knowing the right side does not prove the left.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Layout\\r\\n\\r\\nThe accordion division is wrong.\\r\\n\\r\\n- All 13 sections are closed, and the summaries do not contain the words students will search for. A student looking up `only` must already know it belongs in the right-side group.\\r\\n- The four content types are mixed as peers: indicator lookup, notation, traps, strategy, and practice.\\r\\n- Yes, a pattern panel plus a 19-row table plus an input plus a nested answer is far too much for one section. Group 1 is a lesson module.\\r\\n- Important reference information is rendered at `.78em`, `.85em`, and 10px while framing copy gets more visual weight.\\r\\n- Nested “Check it” accordions create unnecessary tapping.\\r\\n- The unsaved inputs consume space without giving meaningful practice feedback.\\r\\n- “All ten, in four steps” is buried and collapsed even though it is supposed to summarize the page.\\r\\n\\r\\nReorganize it as follows:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Always-visible quick map**\\r\\n\\r\\n   Four compact rows containing the rule, core words, and one example:\\r\\n\\r\\n   - Left: if, when, all, every\\r\\n   - Right: only, only if, must, requires\\r\\n   - Negate left: unless, until, without\\r\\n   - Negate right: no, none, never, cannot\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Tricky wording**\\r\\n\\r\\n   Give `only` versus `the only`, `provided that`, `unless`, and constrained `except` their own annotated examples.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **Full indicator index**\\r\\n\\r\\n   Four accordions whose summaries include the actual words. Put the complete tables here under “See all examples.” Remove the pattern panels.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **Contrapositive and inference**\\r\\n\\r\\n   Put swap/negate/AND↔OR, legal inferences, and the two illegal moves together.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **When to diagram**\\r\\n\\r\\n   Include beginner versus experienced advice, fact-triggering, `few`, `not all`, and one qualified missing-link example.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"a0d262","wall_time_seconds":0.2701443,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":755,"output":"\\r\\nMove all practice inputs and “Test yourself” to The Breakdown, or collect one short practice block at the end.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Redundancy to cut\\r\\n\\r\\n- “The four moves,” four group introductions, four pattern panels, “All ten, in four steps,” and “Test yourself” repeat the same rules.\\r\\n- Rain, pass/attend, enter/pass, and freshman/apply each appear in both a pattern panel and the first table row.\\r\\n- Section 5 repeats `prerequisite`, `depends on`, `will`, and `never`.\\r\\n- Contraposition is explained in Group 3, Section 6, the recap, and the quiz.\\r\\n- Drawing advice appears in Section 10, the recap, and the quiz.\\r\\n- `few`, `only if`, `unless`, and the illegal moves are taught and then immediately retested.\\r\\n- Four “Diagram this” blocks plus the final quiz duplicate the teaching function assigned to The Breakdown.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe clean cut is: one visible quick map, one complete index, one tricky-words section, one contrapositive/inference section, and one qualified drawing section. No files were modified.\\r\\n\\r\\nVERDICT: REVISE\\r\\n\\r\\n### Claude\'s response — triage\\r\\n\\r\\n**VERIFIED TRUE, fixing (real defects):**\\r\\n- **`otherwise` presented as biconditional.** Section 7 says `NOT deposit -> released`\\r\\n  flipped to `NOT released -> deposit` means \\"both directions are covered. Either both\\r\\n  things happen or neither does.\\" That is FALSE: those two are the same rule, a\\r\\n  contrapositive, not a second direction. The file commits the exact reversal error it\\r\\n  warns against two sections later. Highest-severity finding of the round.\\r\\n- **Section 5 \\"Words that are on no list\\" contradicts the tables.** Verified by script:\\r\\n  `prerequisite`, `depends on`, `will`, `never` each appear BOTH in a group table and in\\r\\n  section 5. The section\'s premise is false as written.\\r\\n- **`depends on` arrow invents a term.** \\"The picnic depends on the weather\\" ->\\r\\n  `picnic -> good weather`. Claude found this independently before Codex returned;\\r\\n  independent agreement, so treated as confirmed.\\r\\n\\r\\n**VERIFIED FALSE, rejected:**\\r\\n- **\\"The transcript explicitly teaches `the only` goes left.\\"** Checked every occurrence\\r\\n  of \\"the only\\" in script.md (lines 671, 769, 1109, 1406, 1409, 1522, 1626, 1737).
